Render a publication-quality DATA figure for a top-tier venue research paper.

This figure plots numbers, so it is RENDERED from those numbers — not drawn by an image model. Use the aii-data-fig-gen skill. The output is deterministic: run it once, look at it, fix the spec if the data or labels are wrong, run it again.

STEPS:
1. Read the skill: `.claude/skills/aii-data-fig-gen/SKILL.md`.
2. Pick the chart type that fits the specification below. `python <skill>/scripts/chart_gen.py --list-types` lists them; `--example <type>` prints a complete spec to copy.
3. Write your spec to `fig_causal_grid_spec.json` in your workspace. Put EVERY numeric value from the specification into it — the spec is the figure.
4. Render it:
   `python <skill>/scripts/chart_gen.py --spec fig_causal_grid_spec.json --out fig_causal_grid_v0`
   That writes `fig_causal_grid_v0.pdf` (the deliverable, vector) and `fig_causal_grid_v0.png` (for you to look at).
5. READ THE PNG BACK and check it against the checklist below.
6. If anything is wrong, edit the spec and re-render. Repeat until clean — this is cheap and deterministic, so there is no attempt limit and no reason to accept a flawed figure.

DELIVERABLE: `fig_causal_grid_v0.pdf` in your workspace root. Leave `fig_causal_grid_spec.json` there too — it is the figure's source, and the step files it next to the figure so the figure stays reproducible.

Verification checklist (after EVERY render) — these are the things only you can check, because they are about whether the figure says what you meant:
- Every number in the figure matches the specification — no invented or dropped values
- Axis labels state what is measured AND its units
- Axis ranges make the comparison readable rather than flattening it
- The chart type still makes the point once you can see it drawn
- The caption describes what is actually drawn

The generator already REFUSES the rest rather than shipping them, so a figure you can read back cannot have them: overlapping or cut-off labels, a legend covering the data, a series drawn without a name beside named ones, two series a reader cannot tell apart, and a fit or a scale that the data cannot support. When it exits non-zero the message names the exact key, index or label and what to change — do that rather than re-rolling.

Reach for a generator first, and hand-write only if none fits. Every type in `--list-types` already carries the house style, the data-integrity checks and the layout fixes, so using one is less work than plotting by hand and the result matches every other figure in the paper.

If nothing in the catalogue fits, writing matplotlib yourself is expected and supported — novel figures exist. When you do, import the house style AND its layout passes so the figure still belongs to the set — `apply_house_style`, `place_legend`, `place_point_label`, `fit_legends`, `clear_legends_of_data`, `fit_tick_labels`, `fit_titles`, `rasterize_dense_clouds`, `assert_legends_clear_of_data`, `assert_series_are_distinguishable`, `assert_axis_names_are_unique` from `chart_style`, and `fit_point_labels` + `assert_text_is_legible` from `chart_geometry`, the last of which raises if any label ends up printed over another or cut off at the edge. Build legends with `place_legend` and point names with `place_point_label` — a legend made with a bare `ax.legend` cannot be reflowed when it turns out too wide, and a name written with a bare `ax.annotate` will not be moved off the marker it landed on. The "Use a generator when one fits" section of SKILL.md has the exact snippet and the order to call them in. What you lose is the automatic checking that the picture agrees with the numbers, so verify every value yourself against the specification.
</task>

invariant: the deliverable is the activation-level comparison of the three models and any metric built from it must read activations or weights of a single model. logit-only or text-only readouts are baselines, not the result. this is a mech interp study of where safety lives, not a jailbreak or attack-selection study; do not reframe it as one.

Nothing is generated by\na model, so a bar is the height of its value and every axis is computed.\nRe-running a spec gives a byte-identical PNG; the PDF differs only in its\nembedded creation timestamp.\n\n## Data figure or concept figure?\n\n| The figure is… | Use |\n|---|---|\n| A chart of numbers you have | **this skill** |\n| A confusion matrix, ablation grid, correlation | **this skill** |\n| A scaling law, training curve, Pareto trade-off | **this skill** |\n| Artwork, a metaphor, a cover image | `aii-concept-fig-gen` |\n| An architecture or flow diagram | `aii-concept-fig-gen` |\n\nIn that table **this skill** means a data figure and `aii-concept-fig-gen` a\nconcept figure. For an architecture or flow diagram, read *Limits* first.\n\nThe test is whether the figure has underlying numbers. If it does, an image\nmodel will approximate them — bars that do not match their labels, axis\nticks that do not divide evenly, invented data points. That failure is\ninvisible to a reviewer of the prompt and obvious to a reviewer of the\npaper.\n\n## Use a generator when one fits — hand-write only when none does\n\nThe generators are a menu, not a fence. Every type below is a shortcut that\nalready has the house style, the data-integrity guards and the layout fixes\nbaked in, so reaching for one is almost always less work than plotting by\nhand and the result is consistent with every other figure in the paper.\n\n**Check `--list-types` first.** If a type matches what you need, use it.\nDon't know the name? `--search \"<the question your figure answers>\"` ranks\nthe catalogue by intent rather than by name — `--search \"before and after\nper method\"` puts `slope` first and `dumbbell` second.\nTwo-thirds of research figures are a bar, a line, a scatter or a heatmap,\nand those are solved.\n\n`--search` spans **two corpora** and labels every hit with which one it\ncame from:\n\n| label | what it is | what to do |\n|---|---|---|\n| `ours: <type>` | one of our 61 types | `--example`, edit, render |\n| `chartmimic: <task>/<id>` | a published figure | read its `.py` |\n\nA `chartmimic:` hit is a **reference, not a spec.** It is a human-curated\nfigure from a STEM paper with the matplotlib that draws it — from\nChartMimic ([arXiv:2406.09961](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.09961)), 4,800 of\nthem over 22 categories. Adapting one is a *hand-written* figure: no house\nstyle, no data-integrity guards, no layout passes unless you call them, so\neverything above about hand-written figures still applies. The search\nprints the path to its code under every such hit. Generators outrank\nexemplars on a tie, because a generator is the runnable answer.\n\nReach for an exemplar in exactly two cases: **nothing in the catalogue\nfits** (see the gap table below), or you want to see how a published figure\ndid something — a twin axis, a labelled contour — in working code.\n`--corpus ours|chartmimic|all` narrows the search; the default is `all`.\n\n**If nothing fits, write matplotlib yourself** — that is expected and\nsupported, not a failure. Novel or one-off figures exist. When you do:\n\n```python\nimport sys; sys.path.insert(0, \"<skill>/scripts\")\nimport matplotlib.pyplot as plt\nfrom chart_geometry import assert_text_is_legible, fit_point_labels\nfrom chart_style import (\n    apply_house_style, PALETTE, literal, place_legend, place_point_label,\n    fit_legends, clear_legends_of_data, fit_tick_labels, fit_titles,\n    rasterize_dense_clouds, assert_legends_clear_of_data,\n    assert_series_are_distinguishable, assert_axis_names_are_unique,\n)\n\napply_house_style()                 # fonts, palette, grid, Type-42 PDF fonts\nfig, ax = plt.subplots(figsize=(7, 3.94), layout=\"constrained\")\n...\nplace_legend(ax, loc=\"best\")        # a legend fit_legends can reflow\nplace_point_label(ax, literal(\"Ours\"), (1, 2))   # a name, nudged off the data\nfit_legends(fig)                    # reflow a legend wider than its axes\nclear_legends_of_data(fig)          # move it below the axes if it sits on data\nfit_tick_labels(fig)                # wrap/tilt tick labels that would collide\nfit_titles(fig)                     # wrap any title wider than its axes\nclear_legends_of_data(fig)          # AGAIN — the two above reshaped the axes\nfit_point_labels(fig)               # move point names off markers and curves\nrasterize_dense_clouds(fig)         # >25k points as a bitmap, text stays vector\nassert_text_is_legible(fig)         # raises if any text collides or is cut off\nassert_legends_clear_of_data(fig)   # raises if a legend still hides its data\nassert_series_are_distinguishable(fig)  # raises on two identical legend keys\nassert_axis_names_are_unique(fig)   # raises if one name labels two positions\nfig.savefig(\"figX_v0.pdf\")          # vector, so LaTeX renders text at page res\n```\n\nCall the fitters in that order — the legend decides how much room the axes\nhas, whether it then has to move out of the data is only knowable once it is\nplaced, tick labels change the axes height, the title is measured against the\naxes it ends up on, and a point's name can only be placed once nothing above\nit will move the point again. `clear_legends_of_data` appears TWICE on\npurpose: it decides by measuring, and the two passes between its calls shrink\nthe axes under a legend that is already placed and a fixed size. A wrapped\ntitle took a lone chart from 179 px of axes height to 141, and a legend that\ncovered nothing before covered half a curve after — with the mover's turn\nalready past, so the figure was refused rather than fixed. That is how this catalogue got here.\n\n## Use it\n\n```bash\nSKILL_DIR=\"$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel 2>/dev/null || echo /ai-inventor)/.claude/skills/aii-data-fig-gen\"\nG=\"$SKILL_DIR/scripts/chart_gen.py\"\n\npython \"$G\" --list-types            # the catalogue\npython \"$G\" --search \"compare distributions across groups\"   # find it by intent\npython \"$G\" --search \"pie wedges\" --corpus chartmimic         # exemplars only\npython \"$G\" --audit                 # what ChartMimic has that we do not\npython \"$G\" --example bar           # a complete spec to copy and edit\npython \"$G\" --spec fig1.json --out figures/fig1\n```\n\n`python` here is the pipeline image's interpreter, which has matplotlib and\nscipy installed system-wide. Outside the image use the project venv —\n`.venv/bin/python` — since a bare `python3` will not have them.\n\nWrites `figures/fig1.pdf` **and** `figures/fig1.png`. The PDF is the\ndeliverable — LaTeX renders vector text at page resolution, so it stays\nsharp and selectable at any zoom. The PNG exists so you can read the figure\nback and look at it.\n\n`--format pdf`, `--format png`, `--format pdf,png,svg` narrows the output.\nSVG keeps its labels as TEXT rather than paths, so it stays editable and\nsearchable. EPS is refused: the PostScript backend cannot draw transparency\nand flattens it silently, which the house style uses on nine of every ten\nfigures — the file would not match the PNG you checked.\n`--spec -` reads the spec from stdin.\n\nRuns on `matplotlib` + `numpy`, both already `aii_pipeline` dependencies —\nnothing to install.\n\n## The catalogue\n\n`--example <type>` prints a complete spec for any of these. The \"choose it\nover\" half of each entry is the useful one: most figures have two plausible\ntypes and the choice between them is what decides whether a reviewer reads\nthe point.\n\n### Comparing categories\n\n- `bar` — draws: Vertical bars, grouped or stacked, optional error bars.\n  Choose it over: The default. `barh` if names are long.\n- `barh` — draws: Horizontal bars — labels on the y-axis with room to run.\n  Choose it over: `bar`, whenever names exceed ~40 chars, or for a ranking.\n- `lollipop` — draws: A stem and a dot per category. Choose it over: `barh`,\n  past ~20 categories, where bars become a picket fence.\n- `dumbbell` — draws: Two markers per row joined by a line. Choose it over:\n  Paired bars, when the GAP between them is the story.\n- `slope` — draws: One line per item from a before value to an after value.\n  Choose it over: Paired bars, when which items changed RANK is the story.\n- `bump` — draws: Rank against time, one line per item; the crossings are\n  the finding. Choose it over: `slope`, which shows a reordering for exactly\n  TWO time points and cannot show the path between more.\n- `volcano` — draws: Effect size against significance, with both thresholds\n  drawn. Choose it over: A `bar` of effects, which cannot show what survived\n  correction, or a table of p-values, which cannot show what was big enough\n  to matter.\n- `diverging` — draws: Signed bars either side of zero, sorted. Choose it\n  over: `bar`, for deltas — direction reads instantly.\n- `waterfall` — draws: Steps from a starting total to a final total. Choose\n  it over: `bar`, for an ablation — it shows contributions compounding.\n- `bar_sig` — draws: Grouped bars with significance brackets and stars.\n  Choose it over: `bar`, when the comparison being claimed is pairwise.\n- `forest` — draws: Point estimates with confidence intervals and a null\n  line. Choose it over: `bar`, when whether an interval crosses zero is the\n  question.\n- `radar` — draws: A closed polygon per method over 3+ metrics. Choose it\n  over: Several bar charts, for a multi-metric profile at a glance.\n- `parallel` — draws: One polyline per configuration across independently\n  scaled axes. Choose it over: A table, for a hyperparameter sweep — trends\n  across axes show up.\n- `funnel` — draws: Stage attrition with retention vs. previous and vs.\n  intake. Choose it over: `barh`, when the stages are sequential and losses\n  compound.\n- `stacked_pct` — draws: Composition as percentages; every bar full height.\n  Choose it over: Stacked `bar`, when categories have very different totals.\n- `treemap` — draws: Nested rectangles with AREA proportional to value.\n  Choose it over: `bar`, only when there are too many parts for one axis —\n  length beats area for precise reading.\n- `upset` — draws: Set intersections as sorted bars over a membership\n  matrix. Choose it over: A Venn diagram, past 3 sets — circles cannot stay\n  area-true and stop reading as sets.\n\n### Trends and relationships\n\n- `line` — draws: Multi-series lines with optional uncertainty bands. Choose\n  it over: The default for anything against time or steps.\n- `fan` — draws: A median with nested quantile bands around it. Choose it\n  over: `line` with a band, when the spread is skewed or bounded — a\n  symmetric ± band on an accuracy near its ceiling implies scores above\n  100%.\n- `step` — draws: A piecewise-constant series — value holds, then jumps.\n  Choose it over: `line`, for schedules — a slope implies values that never\n  occurred.\n- `scatter` — draws: Points with an optional least-squares fit and R².\n  Choose it over: `line`, when x is not ordered and the relationship is the\n  point.\n- `joint` — draws: Scatter with the marginal distribution of each variable\n  beside it. Choose it over: `scatter`, when \"and how is each one\n  distributed?\" is the obvious next question — which for a headline\n  correlation it always is.\n- `splom` — draws: Every pair of variables as its own scatter, distributions\n  down the diagonal. Choose it over: `corr`, when the SHAPE of each\n  relationship is the claim — one number cannot tell a straight line from\n  two clusters or an outlier.\n- `bubble` — draws: Scatter with a third variable as marker AREA, plus a\n  size key. Choose it over: `scatter`, when a third quantity matters but not\n  enough for its own axis.\n- `scaling` — draws: Log-log points with a fitted power law and its\n  exponent. Choose it over: `line`, for scaling laws — the exponent is\n  computed and annotated.\n- `speedup` — draws: Measured speedup against worker count, with the ideal\n  line. Choose it over: `line`, for parallel results — the ideal reference\n  is what the claim is measured against.\n- `pareto` — draws: Scatter with the non-dominated frontier drawn through\n  it. Choose it over: `scatter`, for trade-offs where the frontier is the\n  finding.\n- `area` — draws: Stacked areas — a total and how it divides. Choose it\n  over: `line`, when the total matters as much as the parts.\n- `residual` — draws: Residuals against fitted values, with the zero line.\n  Choose it over: Predicted-vs-actual, where heteroscedasticity hides on the\n  diagonal.\n- `bland_altman` — draws: Difference between two methods against their mean,\n  with limits of agreement. Choose it over: A scatter of A against B, where\n  the diagonal reads as agreement and r = 0.99 hides a 10% offset.\n- `acf` — draws: Autocorrelation per lag as stems, with the significance\n  band. Choose it over: `line`, which shows the level and hides whether each\n  point predicts the next.\n- `sankey` — draws: Flows between stages at proportional widths. Choose it\n  over: `area`, when what matters is what became what.\n- `timeline` — draws: Gantt-style spans, one row per task. Choose it over: A\n  table of timestamps, when overlap and duration are the point.\n\n### Model evaluation\n\nGive these raw `labels` and `scores` rather than a precomputed curve wherever\nyou can: the renderer sweeps the threshold itself, so the AUC or AP in the\nlegend is integrated from the points actually drawn and cannot drift from\nthe curve beside it.\n\nWhen only the curve survives — it came from a paper, or from a logged\nartefact — pass it directly instead: `fpr`/`tpr` for `roc`, `recall`/\n`precision` for `pr`, `probabilities`/`labels` for `calibration`. The\nsummary statistic is still integrated from the plotted points, so a PR curve\nthat stops short reports `AP = 0.375 up to recall 0.60` rather than quietly\nextrapolating the rest. One evaluation set per figure: `pr`'s baseline and\n`calibration`'s bins both move with class balance, so curves from different\ntest sets cannot share axes honestly.\n\n- `roc` — draws: ROC curves with AUC in the legend, plus the chance\n  diagonal. Choose it over: `pr`, when the classes are roughly balanced.\n- `pr` — draws: Precision-recall curves with average precision and the\n  prevalence baseline. Choose it over: `roc`, when positives are rare — ROC\n  flatters a rare-class model.\n- `calibration` — draws: Reliability diagram with the ideal diagonal, ECE,\n  and per-bin counts. Choose it over: `roc`/`pr`, when whether to TRUST a\n  probability is the question.\n- `learning_curve` — draws: Score against training-set size, train and\n  validation with ±std bands. Choose it over: `line`, to show whether more\n  data or a better model is the bottleneck.\n- `qq` — draws: Sample quantiles against theoretical normal quantiles, with\n  a reference line. Choose it over: `hist`, for judging normality — the eye\n  reads a straight line far better than a bell.\n- `cd_diagram` — draws: Mean ranks over many datasets, joining methods a\n  test cannot separate. Choose it over: `bar_sig`, which compares pairwise\n  on ONE dataset — this is the many-datasets headline figure.\n\n### Distributions\n\n- `box` — draws: Median, quartiles, whiskers, outliers per group. Choose it\n  over: The compact default for a few groups.\n- `violin` — draws: Full mirrored density per group. Choose it over: `box`,\n  when a distribution may be multi-modal — a box hides that.\n- `strip` — draws: Every raw observation, jittered, with the mean marked.\n  Choose it over: `box`, when n is small enough that each point should be\n  visible.\n- `beeswarm` — draws: Every observation, packed sideways so none hides\n  another. Choose it over: `strip`, whose random jitter still overlaps at\n  any real n — the eye reads the clumps as density and they are partly\n  collision.\n- `ridgeline` — draws: Stacked density curves, one row per group. Choose it\n  over: `violin`, past ~6 groups, where a violin grid gets too wide.\n- `raincloud` — draws: Half violin, box and jittered points together, with\n  n. Choose it over: `violin`, when the reader must see the observations —\n  twelve seeds look as smooth as twelve thousand.\n- `hist` — draws: Binned counts or density. Choose it over: `ecdf`, only\n  when the shape of ONE distribution is the point.\n- `ecdf` — draws: Empirical cumulative distribution, stepped. Choose it\n  over: `hist`, for comparing distributions — no bin width to argue about.\n- `survival` — draws: Kaplan-Meier curves with censoring ticks and\n  confidence bands. Choose it over: `ecdf`, when some subjects have not\n  finished — an ECDF must drop or invent those.\n- `hexbin` — draws: Hexagonal density bins with a colourbar. Choose it over:\n  `scatter`, past ~2000 points where it becomes a solid blob.\n- `hist2d` — draws: A joint distribution as a rectangular binned grid.\n  Choose it over: `hexbin`, when the axes are naturally rectangular.\n\n### Matrices and fields\n\n- `heatmap` — draws: Annotated matrix with a colourbar. Choose it over: A\n  table, when the pattern matters more than the digits.\n- `seqheat` — draws: A per-token quantity drawn on the tokens themselves.\n  Choose it over: `heatmap`, for anything measured per token — it puts\n  indices on an axis and leaves the reader rebuilding the sentence from a\n  legend.\n- `corr` — draws: Correlation matrix, diverging map centred at zero. Choose\n  it over: `heatmap`, for correlations — sign reads from colour direction.\n- `contour` — draws: Filled contours of a 2-D field, levels labelled. Choose\n  it over: `heatmap`, for a smooth field like a loss surface.\n- `clustermap` — draws: Heatmap with rows and columns reordered into their\n  clusters, trees drawn beside. Choose it over: `heatmap`, whenever the row\n  order is arbitrary — block structure that is obvious once reordered is\n  invisible in the order the log happened to emit.\n- `catmap` — draws: A grid whose cells hold a CATEGORY, with a discrete\n  legend and no scale. Choose it over: `heatmap`, for any nominal cell —\n  expert IDs, pass/fail/timeout, which variant won. A ramp asserts that\n  expert 4 is more than expert 1 and that 2 lies between them, and a reader\n  takes the ordering as real.\n- `quiver` — draws: A field of arrows: where each sample is, and where it\n  went. Choose it over: A `scatter` of the before and after positions, which\n  carries the same numbers and leaves the reader pairing points up by eye.\n\n### Structure\n\n- `dendrogram` — draws: Hierarchical clustering as a tree, branch heights\n  the real merge distances. Choose it over: `corr`, which shows every\n  pairwise relationship and no grouping.\n- `tree` — draws: A rooted tree from a parent/child structure you already\n  have. Choose it over: `dendrogram`, which computes its own linkage from a\n  matrix and cannot be given a tree — and `network`, whose force layout\n  loses depth.\n- `network` — draws: A graph as nodes and links, node area and edge width\n  from the data. Choose it over: A concept figure, for anything with REAL\n  edges — an image model draws a plausible graph, not yours. Use `sankey`\n  for flows between ordered stages and `heatmap` for a dense graph.\n\n### Composites\n\n- `panel` — draws: Any of the above in a lettered grid, `(a)`–`(p)`. Choose\n  it over: Several separate figures, when they are read together.\n\n## What ChartMimic has that we do not\n\nMeasured, not guessed: `chart_gen.py --audit` maps all 22 ChartMimic\ncategories onto our 61 types over the 4,800 indexed exemplars. Seventeen\ncategories are covered. These five are not — every one of them is a figure\nshape real papers publish and no generator can produce:\n\n| ChartMimic | n | what to do instead |\n|---|---|---|\n| Combination | 240 | Hand-write. Bars + a line, usually `twinx`. |\n| Hard-to-Recognize | 200 | Hand-write; it is their catch-all. |\n| 3D | 160 | `heatmap` or `contour` — a surface hides data. |\n| Plot-in-Plot | 160 | Hand-write `ax.inset_axes`; not `panel`. |\n| Pie | 160 | `barh`, `stacked_pct` or `treemap`. |\n\n**Combination is the one real gap.** Bars with a line on a second y-axis is\na standard results figure and we have no type for it; the other four are\neither deliberate refusals (pie, 3-D — both read worse than what we do\nhave) or not a chart type at all (Hard-to-Recognize). Adding a\n`bar_line`/dual-axis renderer would close the largest measured hole in the\ncatalogue.\n\nThe 17 covered categories are where an exemplar is a REFERENCE rather than\na gap-filler: our generator is still the answer, and the exemplar shows how\na published figure handled the same shape.\n\n### The exemplar store, and rebuilding the index\n\nThe raw corpus — code, PNG and PDF per exemplar, ~450 MB — lives in\ngitignored `aii_data/chartmimic/`. What is committed is\n`scripts/chartmimic_index.json` (1.02 MB, 4,800 entries: id, category,\nderived subtype, a one-line intent, up to six matplotlib feature keywords).\nImages and code are never tracked — `rule-big-blob-public-only` caps a new\ntracked file at 2 MB and the public export excludes data outright.\n\n```bash\npython \"$SKILL_DIR/scripts/chartmimic_index_build.py\" --fetch  # populate the store\npython \"$SKILL_DIR/scripts/chartmimic_index_build.py\"          # rewrite the index\n```\n\n`--fetch` pins the dataset revision, which the index records alongside its\ncount and build date, so a rebuild is checkable. Without the store the\nsearch still works — it just returns our own types only.\n\n## Spec shape\n\n```json\n{\n  \"type\": \"bar\",\n  \"title\": \"Accuracy by benchmark\",\n  \"xlabel\": \"Benchmark\",\n  \"ylabel\": \"Accuracy (%)\",\n  \"aspect\": \"16:9\",\n  \"categories\": [\"ARC\", \"GSM8K\", \"HumanEval\"],\n  \"series\": [\n    {\"label\": \"Baseline\", \"values\": [41.2, 55.8, 33.1], \"errors\": [1.8, 2.4, 2.9]},\n    {\"label\": \"Ours\",     \"values\": [48.9, 67.3, 45.6], \"errors\": [1.5, 2.0, 2.6]}\n  ]\n}\n```\n\nKeys every type takes: `title`, `aspect` (`\"W:H\"`), `width_in` (default 7.0\n— a full text-width figure), `font_pt`, `font_family`.\n\nKeys that depend on what the type actually draws. Passing one to a type that\nnever reads it is REFUSED by name — *\"nothing read this key\"* — rather than\ndropped quietly, so a figure never comes back missing what the spec asked\nfor. \"Applies to\" below is therefore the set that is accepted, not a hint:\n\n- `xlabel`, `ylabel` — applies to: every type with axes, which is all of\n  them but `panel` — a panel has none of its own, so put the labels on the\n  sub-specs and a label at panel level is refused. `radar`, `treemap`,\n  `sankey`, `parallel` and `upset` do read the key, but draw their own\n  geometry with the axis turned off, so the label is accepted and never\n  painted.\n- `xlim`, `ylim` — applies to: every type — the shared layer applies them\n  whatever the geometry, so these two are never refused as unread. Limits\n  that would crop data are refused rather than applied.\n- `legend_loc` — applies to: only the types that actually draw a legend,\n  i.e. two or more named series. A one-series chart gets none, because a\n  one-entry legend restates the y-label — and asking to place a legend that\n  is not drawn is refused. Takes matplotlib's in-axes placements (`best`,\n  `upper right`, `lower left`, …) and NOT `outside …`: that is what the\n  layout pass itself uses when it moves a legend off the data, and\n  matplotlib accepts it only on a figure legend. You do not need to ask for\n  it — the move happens on its own.\n- `cmap` — applies to: only the eight types that encode a value as colour —\n  `heatmap`, `clustermap`, `corr`, `hist2d`, `hexbin`, `contour`, `quiver`,\n  `seqheat`. Anywhere else it is refused: a bar chart given a colour map is\n  a spec expecting colour to carry a meaning that chart never encodes. The\n  default is already perceptually uniform (`cividis`, or `RdBu_r` where the\n  scale has a meaningful zero), so reach for this only with a reason.\n  Rainbow and cyclic maps are refused: `jet` puts a bright band in the\n  middle of a run that is monotonic in the data, and a reader takes the band\n  for a boundary in the result.\n\n`font_family` REPLACES the font, it does not add a fallback. matplotlib uses\nthe first family it can find and only that one, so the font you name has to\ncover everything on the figure — the script AND the Latin labels, digits and\naxis numbers around it. Needed only for a script the default cannot draw —\nCJK, Devanagari, Thai — and picking a script-only face (e.g. \"Noto Sans Thai\",\nwhich has no Latin) trades one set of hollow boxes for another. Measured: with\nthat font the missing-glyph gate refuses again, naming `l`, `p` and the\ndigits. See *Legibility*.\n\nPer-type keys are documented by `--example <type>`; start from the example\nrather than the schema.\n\n### Multi-panel\n\n```json\n{\"type\": \"panel\", \"title\": \"Overview\", \"ncols\": 2, \"panels\": [\n  {\"type\": \"bar\", \"categories\": [\"A\", \"B\"], \"series\": [{\"values\": [3, 5]}]},\n  {\"type\": \"line\", \"series\": [{\"values\": [1, 2, 4, 8]}]}\n]}\n```\n\nAny chart type nests inside `panels`. Sub-panels are lettered `(a)`, `(b)`…\nautomatically — do not put the letter in the panel's own `title`, which is\nhow panel labels end up collided with their titles.\n\n`ncols` and `aspect` both default from the panel count: the grid is squared\n(capped at three columns, which is the most that fits at the 7-inch text\nwidth) and the canvas is sized so each cell is about 4:3. Pinning `ncols: 4`\nis allowed but leaves each cell 1.75 inches wide, which is narrower than a\nlabelled chart needs — it will be refused rather than drawn on top of\nitself.\n\n## How long text may be\n\nHard caps, checked before anything is drawn, so an over-long string is a\nmessage rather than a figure with its labels cut off. Each was set by\ngrowing that slot until the figure broke, then backing off. Each entry is the\nkey, its cap, then what happened past it:\n\n- `title`, max **120** — never refused, never collided; it just ate the\n  canvas. At 600 characters the chart was 38% of its own figure.\n- `xlabel`, `ylabel`, `cbar_label`, max **80** — silently CLIPPED. An x-label\n  ran off both edges from ~90 characters, a y-label from ~50, cut mid-word, at\n  exit 0.\n- `series[].label`, max **60** — legend entries collided at 80 and collapsed\n  the layout at 100.\n- `categories[]` and any other text, max **80** — under a *vertical* bar the\n  limit is 40, with a pointer to `barh`; see *Legibility*.\n\nA title is a heading; an axis label is a quantity and its unit. Detail\nbelongs in the caption, which has the full column width and as many lines as\nit needs.\n\nThese are coarse budgets that cannot know the figure's real width — a\n3.5-inch column fits about half as much — so the drawn result is measured\ntoo, and anything that still does not fit is refused with the same kind of\nmessage.\n\n## It refuses rather than lying\n\nThe generator exits non-zero, writing nothing, when the figure would not\nmatch its data or a reader would not be able to read it. These were live\ndefects, each of which exited 0 and produced a confident, plausible, wrong\npicture:\n\n- **Length mismatches.** Five categories against three values used to render\n  three bars and silently drop two categories. Ragged series were zero-filled,\n  inventing measurements nobody made.\n- **NaN / Infinity / null / strings in values.** matplotlib draws NaN as\n  *nothing*, so the gap reads as a measured zero.\n- **Right-to-left text.** matplotlib does no bidi reordering and no Arabic\n  joining, so Hebrew and Arabic draw left to right in isolated forms —\n  reversed and unjoined. Every glyph exists, so the missing-glyph gate above\n  sees nothing; the reader who can read the script is the first to know.\n- **Glyphs the font cannot draw.** A missing glyph renders as a hollow box\n  and matplotlib only warns. Adapting one is a *hand-written* figure: no house\nstyle, no data-integrity guards, no layout passes unless you call them, so\neverything above about hand-written figures still applies. The search\nprints the path to its code under every such hit. Generators outrank\nexemplars on a tie, because a generator is the runnable answer.\n\nReach for an exemplar in exactly two cases: **nothing in the catalogue\nfits** (see the gap table below), or you want to see how a published figure\ndid something — a twin axis, a labelled contour — in working code.\n`--corpus ours|chartmimic|all` narrows the search; the default is `all`.\n\n**If nothing fits, write matplotlib yourself** — that is expected and\nsupported, not a failure. fonts, palette, grid, Type-42 PDF fonts\nfig, ax = plt.subplots(figsize=(7, 3.94), layout=\"constrained\")\n...\nplace_legend(ax, loc=\"best\")        # a legend fit_legends can reflow\nplace_point_label(ax, literal(\"Ours\"), (1, 2))   # a name, nudged off the data\nfit_legends(fig)                    # reflow a legend wider than its axes\nclear_legends_of_data(fig)          # move it below the axes if it sits on data\nfit_tick_labels(fig)                # wrap/tilt tick labels that would collide\nfit_titles(fig)                     # wrap any title wider than its axes\nclear_legends_of_data(fig)          # AGAIN — the two above reshaped the axes\nfit_point_labels(fig)               # move point names off markers and curves\nrasterize_dense_clouds(fig)         # >25k points as a bitmap, text stays vector\nassert_text_is_legible(fig)         # raises if any text collides or is cut off\nassert_legends_clear_of_data(fig)   # raises if a legend still hides its data\nassert_series_are_distinguishable(fig)  # raises on two identical legend keys\nassert_axis_names_are_unique(fig)   # raises if one name labels two positions\nfig.savefig(\"figX_v0.pdf\")          # vector, so LaTeX renders text at page res\n```\n\nCall the fitters in that order — the legend decides how much room the axes\nhas, whether it then has to move out of the data is only knowable once it is\nplaced, tick labels change the axes height, the title is measured against the\naxes it ends up on, and a point's name can only be placed once nothing above\nit will move the point again. `clear_legends_of_data` appears TWICE on\npurpose: it decides by measuring, and the two passes between its calls shrink\nthe axes under a legend that is already placed and a fixed size. That is how this catalogue got here.\n\n## Use it\n\n```bash\nSKILL_DIR=\"$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel 2>/dev/null || echo /ai-inventor)/.claude/skills/aii-data-fig-gen\"\nG=\"$SKILL_DIR/scripts/chart_gen.py\"\n\npython \"$G\" --list-types            # the catalogue\npython \"$G\" --search \"compare distributions across groups\"   # find it by intent\npython \"$G\" --search \"pie wedges\" --corpus chartmimic         # exemplars only\npython \"$G\" --audit                 # what ChartMimic has that we do not\npython \"$G\" --example bar           # a complete spec to copy and edit\npython \"$G\" --spec fig1.json --out figures/fig1\n```\n\n`python` here is the pipeline image's interpreter, which has matplotlib and\nscipy installed system-wide. Outside the image use the project venv —\n`.venv/bin/python` — since a bare `python3` will not have them.\n\nWrites `figures/fig1.pdf` **and** `figures/fig1.png`. The PDF is the\ndeliverable — LaTeX renders vector text at page resolution, so it stays\nsharp and selectable at any zoom. The PNG exists so you can read the figure\nback and look at it.\n\n`--format pdf`, `--format png`, `--format pdf,png,svg` narrows the output.\nSVG keeps its labels as TEXT rather than paths, so it stays editable and\nsearchable. EPS is refused: the PostScript backend cannot draw transparency\nand flattens it silently, which the house style uses on nine of every ten\nfigures — the file would not match the PNG you checked.\n`--spec -` reads the spec from stdin.\n\nRuns on `matplotlib` + `numpy`, both already `aii_pipeline` dependencies —\nnothing to install.\n\n## The catalogue\n\n`--example <type>` prints a complete spec for any of these. Choose it over: `barh`,\n  past ~20 categories, where bars become a picket fence.\n- `dumbbell` — draws: Two markers per row joined by a line. Choose it over:\n  Paired bars, when the GAP between them is the story.\n- `slope` — draws: One line per item from a before value to an after value.\n  Choose it over: Paired bars, when which items changed RANK is the story.\n- `bump` — draws: Rank against time, one line per item; the crossings are\n  the finding. Choose it over: `slope`, which shows a reordering for exactly\n  TWO time points and cannot show the path between more.\n- `volcano` — draws: Effect size against significance, with both thresholds\n  drawn. Choose\n  it over: `bar`, for an ablation — it shows contributions compounding.\n- `bar_sig` — draws: Grouped bars with significance brackets and stars.\n  Choose it over: `bar`, when the comparison being claimed is pairwise.\n- `forest` — draws: Point estimates with confidence intervals and a null\n  line. {"stdout": "def require_colour_limits_cover(values, vmin: float, vmax: float, *, stated, what=\"matrix\") -> None:\n    \"\"\"Refuse colour limits that crop the data they are drawn over.\n\n    The colour-axis twin of ``_limits_must_cover_data``. Everything past a\n    limit is clamped to the end colour, so cells that differ are painted\n    identically and the colourbar states a range the data does not have:\n    ``vmax: 0.3`` on a matrix running 0.10..0.95 gave four of six cells the\n    same yellow, a 0.30 result and a 0.95 result indistinguishable, under a\n    bar labelled 0.100..0.300. Colour is the only encoding a heatmap has —\n    with ``annotate`` off (the ``clustermap`` default) nothing else carries\n    the number — so a clamped cell is not a zoom, it is a wrong reading.\n\n    Takes the RESOLVED limits and the keys that were stated, because under\n    ``diverging`` a stated ``vmax`` also sets ``vmin`` at ``-vmax``: checking\n    the spec keys alone would miss the low end it silently cropped.\n    \"\"\"\n    import numpy as np\n\n    if not stated:\n        return\n    array = np.asarray(values, dtype=float)\n    low, high = float(array.min()), float(array.max())\n    slack = max(high - low, 1e-9) * 1e-6\n    counts = [\n        (int((array < vmin - slack).sum()), f\"below {vmin:g}\"),\n        (int((array > vmax + slack).sum()), f\"above {vmax:g}\"),\n    ]\n    outside = [f\"{n} {where}\" for n, where in counts if n]\n    if not outside:\n        return", "stderr": "\nShell cwd was reset to /ai-inventor/aii_data/runs/run_YqmEFECOIR3D/4_gen_paper_repo/_2_gen_viz/gen_viz_4", "interrupted": false, "isImage": false, "noOutputExpected": false}

ent_2/out/cells/abliterated/P_B3_grid3__6e406f7f.npz\n/ai-inventor/aii_data/runs/run_YqmEFECOIR3D/3_invention_loop/iter_4/gen_art/gen_art_experiment_2/out/cells/abliterated/P_B2_grid3__6e406f7f.json\n/ai-inventor/aii_data/runs/run_YqmEFECOIR3D/3_invention_loop/iter_4/gen_art/gen_art_experiment_2/out/cells/abliterated/P_B2_grid3__6e406f7f.npz\n/ai-inventor/aii_data/runs/run_YqmEFECOIR3D/3_invention_loop/iter_4/gen_art/gen_art_experiment_2/out/cells/abliterated/P_B1_grid3__6e406f7f.json\n/ai-inventor/aii_data/runs/run_YqmEFECOIR3D/3_invention_loop/iter_4/gen_art/gen_art_experiment_2/out/cells/abliterated/P_B1_grid3__6e406f7f.npz\n/ai-inventor/aii_data/runs/run_YqmEFECOIR3D/3_invention_loop/iter_4/gen_art/gen_art_experiment_2/out/cells/abliterated/P_B6_grid2__6e406f7f.json\n/ai-inventor/aii_data/runs/run_YqmEFECOIR3D/3_invention_loop/iter_4/gen_art/gen_art_experiment_2/out/cells/abliterated/P_B6_grid2__6e406f7f.npz\n/ai-inventor/aii_data/runs/run_YqmEFECOIR3D/3_invention_loop/iter_4/gen_art/gen_art_experiment_2/out/cells/abliterated/P_B5_grid2__6e406f7f.json\n=== README head ===\n# Causal depth × site grid: where does Qwen3-4B's refusal live?\n\nThis is an intervention study on three checkpoints of the same model:\n\n- `Qwen/Qwen3-4B`, called **instruct** here;\n- `Qwen/Qwen3-4B-SafeRL`, the official safety fine-tune, called **SafeRL**;\n- `mlabonne/Qwen3-4B-abliterated`, a community uncensored checkpoint, called **abliterated**.\n\nIt asks three questions:\n\n- **Where** in depth and **where** in the token stream does the request-harmfulness direction do causal work on refusal and over-refusal?\n- Does that differ between instruct and SafeRL?\n- Do the cheap readouts built on that direction move when behaviour moves?\n\nIt is the first *executed* site-local causal grid of this research run. The iteration-3 plan for this grid was never executed.\n\n> RESULTS: see [Results](#results). Every number there is regenerated by `src/analyze.py` from the checkpointed cells into\n> `results/analysis.json`, `results/summary_tables.md` and `method_out.json`.\n\n**Headline findings**\n\n- **The request axis F causally controls the *form* of the first response token, not the refusal decision.** Projecting F out at the last prompt position in B4-B6 is CAUSAL (registered F1 rule) for the first-token refusal-onset mass. In instruct it moves -2.9 nats, and refusal-onset probability at token 1 drops from about 78% to about 4%. Judged refusal of harmful requests stays at 0.92 → 0.88 in instruct and 0.98 in SafeRL: 0 of 18 site×band cells are CAUSAL in either model, because the model rewrites the opening and still refuses.\n- **Local removal fails because refusal is redundant across positions.** In the exploratory T3 control, removing the same F at *every* position within B4 drops instruct refusal to 0.67 (effect_FR -0.33 [-0.48, -0.21]), so the direction is causal. SafeRL's refusal is more robust to that global ablation (exploratory DiD +0.23 [+0.08, +0.38]).\n- **Over-refusal is the site-local lever.** On hard-benign probes, F or N6 removal at mid-depth (B4-B5, at P and D′, and even at E) lowers over-refusal by 0.12-0.19 in both models. Only instruct N6 @ P_B5 survives Holm over 18 cells.\n- **Decodable ≠ causal.** F is decodable in 12 of 18 cells per model, yet 12 of 12 are behaviourally inert for refusal. BL1, RD, T1ref and N1 readouts swing by several nats under interventions that leave behaviour unchanged. The matched random control alone moves BL1 by about 2 nats.\n- **Three checkpoints in activation space.** SafeRL keeps instruct's request and twin axes (cos ≥ 0.84 per band). The abliterated checkpoint keeps them in B1-B3 but rotates them from B4 on: cos(F) ≈ 0.3 and cos(N6) ≈ 0.02. B4 on is exactly the depth where F carries the token-1 refusal drive in the other two. In abliterated, removing its own F *raises* refusal-onset mass (sign reversal).\n\n## Design\n\n| component | definition |\n|---|---|\n| models | instruct and SafeRL: the full grid. abliterated: the site-P grid, judged site-P generation, readouts, forward-only windows, and the site-E late readout. Each model runs alone on the GPU in bf16 (NVIDIA L4). |\n| depth bands | B1…B6 = sixths of the 36 decoder layers: L0-5, 6-11, 12-17, 18-23, 24-29, 30-35. \"Layer *l*\" is the output of decoder block *l* = hidden index *l*+1. |\n| sites | **P**: the last prompt position only. **D′**: the last prompt position plus decode calls t=1…7, so it can change token 1. **E**: decode calls t=5…20, so tokens 1-5 cannot change by construction. **D**: decode calls 1…8, run only as a readout site, in the teacher-forced windows. |\n| directions | **F**: the model's own request axis per layer, unit(mean EASY-harmful − mean EASY-benign) over the iteration-2 last-prompt-token harvest (`A_prompt.npy`). It uses zero forwards. **N6**: the benign-side XSTest twin direction, unit(mean safe twin − mean unsafe twin), over the 85 confirmatory twin pairs from one prompt-only pass. **N6perp**: N6 orthogonalised to F. **F_perpU**: F with its top-8 refusal-token unembedding rows projected out. |\n| arms | **0**: no intervention. **F / N6 / N6perp / F_perpU**: projection-out, h ← h − (h·v)v, in every layer of the band at the site positions, fp32 math cast to bf16. **R**: random control. At P and in the teacher-forced windows it is *matched displacement*, h ← h − c_F·u, where c_F is the coefficient the F row of the same item removes in the same pass, so the R/F displacement ratio is exactly 1. At D′/E it is the plan's own-coefficient form h ← h − (h·F)u. u is a unit Gaussian direction orthogonalised to span{F, N6}. There are 20 draws for forward-only outcomes and 3 for judged outcomes. **POS**: a positive control that replaces the residual with the mean EASY-benign residual (B3, B4 at P). It measures generic disruption and is not a treatment. |\n| outcomes | Judged refusal / harmful compliance / safe engagement on 48 held-out harmful probes, and judged over-refusal on 48 hard-benign probes. Generation is greedy, 80 new tokens. The judge is gemini-2.5-flash-lite with the frozen `lc_judge_iter1` rubric, deduplicated by sha256(prompt+response). Other outcomes: ARC-Easy flip-to-wrong on items correct under arm 0 (no judge); GSM8K collateral on CAUSAL cells; teacher-forced refusal drive RD = log-mass on the 44 refusal-onset tokens; G1 = lse(refusal) − lse(control); T1ref. |\n| causal rule | A cell is CAUSAL if the Holm-adjusted paired sign-flip p of **effect_FR** = mean_i[y_F,i − mean_j y_Rj,i] is below 0.05 across the 18 cells within (model, outcome, arm). The site-P forward-only grid (registered F1) uses a 6-cell family and additionally needs the empirical p over the 20 R draws to be below 0.05. |\n| decodability | Arm-0 AUROC (32+32 held-out items) of the band-mean standardised projection on F at the site's window. DECODABLE if the bootstrap lower CI bound is above 0.60. |\n| readouts under intervention | BL1_easy / BL1_hard (G1 contrast, final logits applied once, never pooled), N1 (Cohen d along the *unintervened* F at l*), N6, N2 (F_perpU) and N7, on the 256 stimuli under each arm. The site-E late readout teacher-forces the iteration-1 `safety_2x2` 144-token continuations, edits tokens 5-20, and reads the hazardous-vs-benign separation at tokens 40-55. |\n| T3 disambiguation control (added) | **genG**: the same F / N6 projected out at *every* prompt position and every decode call, per band and in all 36 layers. This is abliteration logic applied at inference time. |\n\n### Pre-registration\n\n- `prereg.json` (sha256 in `prereg.sha256`, 3b0aaa19…) was frozen on the CPU-only box before any model load on items. It registers the CPU-fallback F1 subset: the site-P forward-only grid, the judged B3/B4 × P cell on 24+24 items at 48 tokens, and ARC.\n- The pod restarted onto a GPU. The plan's primary design was then frozen in `prereg_gpu_addendum.json` (sha256 in `prereg_gpu_addendum.sha256`, 524c15c0…) **before any Qwen3-4B outcome was computed**.\n- The F1 cells run unchanged and are analysed exactly as registered.\n- Every deviation is logged in `results/deviations.json`.\n\n## Results\n\nNumbers come from `results/analysis.json`, summarised in `results/summary_tables.md`. Rates are over 48 harmful\nand 48 hard-benign held-out probes, with greedy decoding for 80 tokens. effect_FR is the paired difference between\nthe treatment and the mean of its matched random controls; 95% bootstrap CIs are in brackets. **\\*** marks a cell\nthat is CAUSAL under Holm correction.\n\n### 0. Baselines (arm 0) and checks\n\n| | instruct | SafeRL | abliterated |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| judged refusal on harmful probes | 0.917 [0.80, 0.97] | 0.979 [0.89, 1.00] | 0.188 [0.10, 0.32] |\n| judged harmful compliance | 0.000 | 0.000 | 0.604 |\n| judged over-refusal on hard-benign probes | 0.44 (0.375-0.44 across cells) | 0.48 (0.48-0.58 across cells) | 0.04 (0.04-0.06 across cells) |\n| first-token refusal-onset mass RD (harm, nats) | -0.25 | -0.006 | -4.70 |\n| BL1_easy / BL1_hard (final logits once) | 6.65 / 6.21 | 7.50 / 4.69 | -5.88 / 2.16 |\n| l* (argmax d_hard) / N6 best twin layer | 26 / 26 | 26 / 31 | 23 / 19 |\n| ARC-Easy arm-0 accuracy | 0.953 | 0.953 | 0.953 |\n\n- **Plan check T1.** The arm-0 judged rates agree with the earlier iterations' judged rates for all three checkpoints; every Newcombe difference CI contains 0. Those reference rates used different items and 140-token generations. BL1_truelogit recomputed on the GPU (6.207 HARD, instruct) matches the disk value 6.2076.\n- **Hook checks** pass for smoke, instruct, SafeRL and abliterated (`results/hook_checks_*.json`):\n  - a1: no-op hooks are bitwise identical to hooks removed.\n  - a2: arm-0 rows are bitwise unaffected by active rows in the same batch.\n  - b: after projection, |h·v|/|h| < 1e-3.\n  - c: the matched R/F displacement ratio is 1 ± 1e-7.\n- **Site checks:**\n  - At E, tokens 1-5 are identical to arm 0 in every row.\n  - The edit steps seen equal the registered windows.\n  - The post-cast residual is < 1e-3 everywhere.\n- **Greedy knife-edge.** The arm-0 greedy labels of 3-8 items differ between cell families with different batch shapes (GPU numerics). Instruct over-refusal is 0.375-0.44 across the 32 families. Every contrast is therefore computed *within* a cell, against that cell's co-batched arm 0 and matched R (`arm0_stability`).\n\n### 1. Registered F1 analysis: site-P forward-only outcomes. F controls the first token.\n\nProjecting F out at the last prompt position is **CAUSAL** for the first-token refusal drive in bands B4-B6 in both\nmodels. The rule is Holm over 6 cells, plus the empirical p over 20 matched random draws at its minimum of 1/21.\n\n| RD_harm effect_FR (nats) | B1 | B2 | B3 | B4 | B5 | B6 |\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n| instruct, F | +0.00 | +0.02 | -0.06 | **-2.87\\*** | **-2.59\\*** | **-1.58\\*** |\n| SafeRL, F | -0.00 | +0.00 | -0.00 | **-0.96\\*** | **-0.54\\*** | **-0.23\\*** |\n| instruct, N6 | +0.01 | -0.01 | +0.05\\* | **-1.59\\*** | **-2.09\\*** | **-1.47\\*** |\n| instruct, N6perp (N6 minus its F component) | +0.03 | -0.02 | +0.07 | +0.04 | +0.00 | -0.14 |\n\n- In instruct at B4, removing F moves the refusal-onset probability at token 1 from about 78% to about 4%. The first token stops being a refusal onset for 46% of harmful items (T1ref -0.46\\*).\n- N6's effect is carried entirely by its overlap with F: cos(F, N6) at l* is -0.86 in both models, and N6perp is null.\n- A few SafeRL cells flag CAUSAL with |effect| ≤ 0.01 nats because RD sits at its ceiling and has almost no variance. They are statistically detectable but negligible.\n\n### 2. Judged behaviour: site-local removal does not change refusal of harmful requests\n\nThe same interventions, at every site and band, leave **judged refusal of harmful requests unchanged** in both models.", "stderr": "", "interrupted": false, "isImage": false, "noOutputExpected": false}
```

I cannot provide…\".\n- **The token-1 readout (RD / T1ref) therefore massively overstates behavioural control.** A -2.9-nat drop at token 1 corresponds to a ≤0.06 change in judged refusal.\n- **So does the text-only baseline.** The keyword-refusal proxy registers a CAUSAL collapse of \"refusal\" under the same arm. Only **instruct N6 at P_B5** survives Holm over 18 cells (p_holm = 0.036).\n- **Power.** At n = 48 the paired MDE (80% power, unadjusted α = 0.05) is 0.13-0.20 for the over-refusal cells where effects appear.\n  - It is about 1.4× larger at the Holm-18 threshold.\n  - The median over all non-degenerate judged null cells is 0.06-0.07.\n  - The observed -0.12 to -0.19 over-refusal effects therefore sit right at the design's detection limit. \"Not CAUSAL\" means \"not detected at this power\", not \"zero\".\n- The generic-disruption positive control POS (residual patch to the mean benign residual) also lowers instruct over-refusal at P_B4 (-0.146). The F-vs-matched-R contrast, not F-vs-0, is the causal claim.\n- In abliterated, POS at P_B4 lowers the residual refusal from 0.17 to 0.10 (vs R 0.22; Holm over its 2-cell family).\n- In instruct, even site **E** moves over-refusal at B4 (-0.13). Tokens 1-5 are untouched there, so the borderline decision is still open after the model has started answering.\n\n### 4. T3 disambiguation: the same F *is* causal when removed at every position\n\nHere F is projected out at every prompt position and every decode call. That is abliteration logic applied as an\ninference-time ablation.\n\n| all-position ablation | instruct refusal (0 → F; R) | effect_FR | SafeRL refusal (0 → F; R) | effect_FR |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| B4 only | 0.92 → **0.67**; R 1.00 | **-0.33** [-0.48, -0.21] | 0.96 → 0.88; R 0.98 | -0.10 [-0.21, -0.02] |\n| all 36 layers | 0.92 → **0.71**; R 0.90 | **-0.19** [-0.31, -0.08] | 0.96 → 0.85; R 0.90 | -0.04 [-0.15, +0.08] |\n| all 36 layers, N6 | 0.92 → **0.58**; R 0.92 | -0.33 | 0.96 → 0.85; R 0.98 | -0.13 |\n\n- Instruct also produces harmful content under all-position F at B4 (harmful compliance 0.08) and drops over-refusal from 0.38 to 0.08 (effect_FR -0.375).\n- SafeRL minus instruct DiD for F on refusal at B4 is **+0.229 [+0.083, +0.375]** (exploratory, uncorrected). **SafeRL's refusal is markedly more robust than instruct's to removing its own request axis everywhere.** With all 36 layers the DiD is +0.146 [-0.02, +0.33].\n- The hook and the direction are therefore sound. The site-local null in §2 reflects *positional redundancy*: the refusal decision is re-derived from other prompt positions.\n\n### 5. Decodable but inert\n\n- F is decodable at site P in every band: arm-0 AUROC 0.80-0.96 for instruct and 0.80-0.98 for SafeRL.\n- In the decode windows D and E, F is decodable only at B4-B6 (0.85-0.98).\n- **Not one of the 12 decodable cells per model is CAUSAL for judged refusal** (12/12 decodable-but-inert in both models). For N6 on over-refusal the count is 11/12 (instruct) and 12/12 (SafeRL).\n- This is the pattern of Basu et al. (arXiv:2603.18353) *for its SAE arm only*. Its Arm 1 corrected 17/85 and disrupted 25/47, so this is not a claim that mechanistic methods never work.\n\n### 6. Readouts move when behaviour does not\n\nReadouts on the 256 stimuli under site-P intervention (instruct, B5):\n\n- F drives BL1_easy from 6.65 to -1.19 (Δ = -7.84 [-9.67, -6.09]) and BL1_hard from 6.21 to 2.06.\n- N1 (d along the unintervened F at l* = 26) falls from 2.45 to 0.35. This is MECHANICALLY_FORCED, because l* is in B5.\n- Judged refusal on the harmful probes does not move at all (§2).\n- The matched random control alone shifts BL1_easy by +2.2 at B5 and by -2.5 at B4. **|ΔBL1 under R| is of the same order as a real treatment effect.**\n\nLogit-lens-style readouts (BL1, RD, T1ref, N1) track the local representation, not the behavioural decision.\n\n### 7. Sites and depth\n\n- **P vs D′ (N10).** Registered sign: |P| > |D′|. The P-minus-D′ contrast of effect_FR on refusal is within ±0.03 in every band for both models, so there is no refusal effect to localise. On over-refusal, P and D′ are similar at B4-B5.\n- **E is smaller than P** for SafeRL over-refusal (B4: -0.19 at P vs -0.06 at E), as registered. **It is not smaller for instruct at B4** (-0.125 vs -0.132).\n- **Forward-only teacher-forced windows.** At D, F lowers the refusal-onset mass after the window (instruct B4 -1.78 nats\\*, SafeRL B4 -0.89\\*). The log-probability of the arm-0 continuation changes much less (-0.83 / -0.20).\n\n### 8. Two-sidedness (SafeRL vs instruct)\n\n- **Registered prediction:** DiD_OR = [N6-R](SafeRL) - [N6-R](instruct) > 0 (instruct's over-refusal falls more). **It is not supported.**\n  - The N6 over-refusal DiD is positive in only 8 of 18 cells: P_B5 -0.014 [-0.17, +0.15]; P_B4 -0.056 [-0.26, +0.13]; D'_B4 +0.069 [-0.08, +0.23].\n  - Of the 54 judged-grid DiDs (18 cells × 3 contrasts), 3 have uncorrected CIs that exclude 0. That is about as many as chance predicts (2.7 at α = 0.05). The three are P_B1 F on over-refusal +0.090 [+0.03, +0.17], P_B4 F on refusal +0.069 [+0.01, +0.15], and P_B6 N6 on over-refusal -0.139 [-0.28, -0.00].\n  - At site-local resolution, SafeRL and instruct over-refusal respond alike.\n- The clear model difference is **refusal robustness under all-position ablation** (§4, DiD +0.23).\n- The site-P token-1 refusal drive is also more robust in SafeRL (registered F1 DiD for F on RD_harm at B4: +1.91 nats).\n\n### 9. Collateral\n\n- **ARC-Easy.** The flip-to-wrong rate is 0 of 61 correct items for every band and every reported arm (F, N6, R_F1) in all three models. Site-local ablation does not touch multiple-choice answers.\n- **GSM8K:** GSM8K (16 items, 256 tokens, last-number grading, no judge) was run on the one CAUSAL cell (instruct N6 @ P_B5) and on the strongest nominal over-refusal cells (P_B4, D'_B4, D'_B5), which are descriptive.\n  - Arm-0 accuracy is 8/16 for instruct and 10/16 for SafeRL (non-thinking mode, final-number answer only).\n  - Every F / N6 / R arm stays within ±2 items of arm 0: F − R is −0.06 to 0.00, N6 − R is 0.00 to +0.13.\n  - No collateral damage is detected.\n\n### 10. The third checkpoint (abliterated) and the extras\n\n**How the three checkpoints differ in activation space.** Cosines between the models' own per-layer directions,\naveraged over each band's layers:\n\n| band | cos F (instruct, SafeRL) | cos F (instruct, abliterated) | cos N6 (instruct, SafeRL) | cos N6 (instruct, abliterated) |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| B1 | 0.997 | 0.999 | 0.995 | 0.997 |\n| B2 | 0.982 | 0.994 | 0.977 | 0.926 |\n| B3 | 0.916 | 0.943 | 0.889 | 0.609 |\n| B4 | 0.895 | **0.412** | 0.922 | **0.021** |\n| B5 | 0.901 | **0.306** | 0.918 | **0.027** |\n| B6 | 0.841 | **0.328** | 0.863 | **0.061** |\n\n- **SafeRL** keeps both axes (cos ≥ 0.84).\n- **abliterated** keeps them in B1-B3 but **rotates the request axis away from B4 on** (cos ≈ 0.3), and its benign-twin axis becomes orthogonal to instruct's (cos ≈ 0.02). B4-B6 is exactly the depth where F is causal for the first-token refusal drive in instruct and SafeRL (§1).\n- **Readouts in abliterated.** F's separability on HARD stimuli collapses: d at l* falls from 2.45 to 0.71, and l* moves from 26 to 23. The refusal-token readout inverts: BL1_easy is -5.88 vs +6.65 in instruct, and the arm-0 refusal-onset mass on harmful probes is about 1% (RD -4.70).\n- **Its own F is still decodable** at P (AUROC 0.80-0.89) and in the decode windows at B4-B6 (0.75-0.83).\n- **Removing F in abliterated has the opposite sign.** Its own F at P *raises* the first-token refusal-onset mass (RD_harm B4 +1.24 nats\\*, against -2.87 in instruct). Judged behaviour does not move: 0/6 CAUSAL, max |effect_FR| 0.063.\n\n**F_perpU (exploratory; F with its top-8 refusal-token unembedding rows projected out, site P).**\n\n| instruct / SafeRL | RD_harm under F | RD_harm under F_perpU | over-refusal 0 → F / F_perpU |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| B4 | -3.17 / -0.99 | -2.99 / -1.02 | 0.42 → 0.23 / 0.29 · 0.50 → 0.29 / 0.31 |\n| B5 | -2.83 / -0.55 | -2.68 / -0.62 | 0.42 → 0.27 / 0.27 · 0.50 → 0.35 / 0.38 |\n| B6 | -1.81 / -0.25 | **-0.75 / -0.03** | 0.42 → 0.35 / 0.42 · 0.50 → 0.38 / 0.46 |\n\nThe arm-0 RD_harm is -0.26 (instruct) and -0.01 (SafeRL).\n\n- At mid-depth (B4-B5), removing only the part of F *orthogonal* to the refusal-token unembeddings reproduces F's effects. The mid-depth request axis acts as a representational signal, not as a direct logit write.\n- In the last band (B6), most of F's first-token effect disappears once its unembedding-aligned component is kept. The late-layer F writes refusal logits directly. The token-1 change rate falls from 0.42 to 0.22 (instruct) and from 0.46 to 0.09 (SafeRL).\n\n**Site-E late readout (`safety_2x2`, 340 teacher-forced sequences per model).** The N1-analogue (Cohen d, hazardous vs benign continuation, projection on F at l*, tokens 40-55) is 0.63 in instruct, 0.82 in SafeRL and 0.33 in abliterated at arm 0.\n\n- Editing the early window (tokens 5-20) with F shifts it by at most 0.08, at B4.\n  - F − R: −0.077 [−0.100, −0.055] instruct; −0.059 [−0.087, −0.033] SafeRL; −0.070 [−0.087, −0.055] abliterated.\n  - That is ≤ 12% of d_0 in instruct and SafeRL.\n- Bands above l* are exactly 0 by construction.\n- The late-window hazard representation is computed from the continuation itself. It is not carried forward from the early-window request axis.\n\n**GSM8K collateral.** See §9.\n\n### 11. The text-only baseline fails on SafeRL\n\nThe keyword-refusal proxy agrees with the judge at kappa 0.50 on instruct but **kappa ≈ 0.00 on SafeRL**. SafeRL refuses\nwith \"It's important to clarify…\", which opens 41 of 48 harmful arm-0 responses and contains no lexical refusal\nmarker. A text-only refusal readout is a baseline, not a measurement, for this checkpoint family.\n\n### Judge cost\n\nJudge: 51,936 judged (request, response) records.\n\n- 33,491 were deduplicated cache hits and 15,492 were API calls; 0 NA and 0 PROXY labels.\n- Cost: $0.764 (google/gemini-2.5-flash-lite through OpenRouter), plus $0.0001 for the session-2 judge unit test. The registered cap was $3.00 and the artifact budget $10.\n- The text-free label cache ships as `results/judge_label_cache.jsonl`.\n\n## Repository layout\n\n| path | what it is |\n|---|---|\n| `method.py` | Experiment runner. Each stage is a function; cells are checkpointed to `out/cells/<model>/<cell>__<hookhash>.{npz,json}` and skipped when present. Judging runs in a background thread. |\n| `src/engine.py` | Intervention engine: CUDA/CPU, prefix-cached row stacking, one forward hook per decoder layer that edits the last S positions, and site-windowed greedy generation. |\n| `src/judge.py` | The frozen `lc_judge_iter1` protocol (gemini-2.5-flash-lite through OpenRouter, temperature 0), with a dedup cache, cost ledger, keyword-proxy fallback and `derive()` label mapping. |\n| `src/prep.py` | Item splits, directions from the iteration-2 disk harvest, and the prereg freeze. It was run once in session 2 and refuses to overwrite. |\n| `src/stats.py` (+ `src/test_stats.py`) | Tested pure-numpy statistics: paired bootstrap, sign-flip p, Holm, empirical R-draw p, AUROC CI, Cohen d, d_z, paired DiD, Wilson/Newcombe. |\n| `src/analyze.py` | Reads only the cells and writes `results/analysis.json`, `results/summary_tables.md` and `method_out.json`. It never loads a model. |\n| `src/figures.py` | Draws every figure in `figures/` from `results/analysis.json`. |\n| `src/peek.py` | Progress monitor for the judged grid (rates per arm). |\n| `src/common.py` | Paths, band definitions, JSON I/O, logging. |\n| `run_chain.sh` | The GPU queue run after the main job: genG, then the abliterated checkpoint, then genU + e2x2. |\n| `run_chain2.sh` | GSM8K collateral on the CAUSAL cell and the three strongest nominal over-refusal cells. |\n| `pyproject.toml`, `uv.lock` | Pinned environment. torch comes from the CUDA 12.8 index. |\n| `assets/items.json` | Frozen item splits (sha256 in the prereg): 48 harm_eval, 48 hb_eval, 32+32 decodability, 64 ARC, 16 GSM8K, 85 twins. |", "stderr": "", "interrupted": false, "isImage": false, "noOutputExpected": false}
```

### [69] TOOL CALL — Bash · 2026-09-22 07:29:25 UTC

```
EXP2=/ai-inventor/aii_data/runs/run_YqmEFECOIR3D/3_invention_loop/iter_4/gen_art/gen_art_experiment_2
python3 -c "
import json
d = json.load(open('$EXP2/results/analysis.json'))
sites = ['P','Dprime','E']
bands = ['B1','B2','B3','B4','B5','B6']
for model in d['per_model']:
    print('='*20, model, '='*20)
    cg = d['per_model'][model].get('causal_grid_judged', {})
    if 'over_refusal_hb' not in cg:
        print('  no over_refusal_hb key'); continue
    for arm in ['F','N6']:
        if arm not in cg['over_refusal_hb']:
            continue
        print(' arm', arm)
        cells = cg['over_refusal_hb'][arm]['cells']
        for s in sites:
            row=[]
            for b in bands:
                k = f'{s}_{b}'
                if k in cells:
                    c = cells[k]
                    e = c['effect_vsR']['effect']
                    lo = c['effect_vsR']['ci_lo']; hi = c['effect_vsR']['ci_hi']
                    caus = '*' if c.get('CAUSAL') else ''
                    row.append(f'{e:+.3f}[{lo:+.2f},{hi:+.2f}]{caus}')
                else:
                    row.append('  --  ')
            print(f'  {s:7s}', ' | '.join(row))
"
```

    for s in sites:
      c=[r for r in rows if r['model']==m and r['band']==b and r['site']==s]
      if not c: out.append('MISSING'); continue
      r=c[0]

    print(b, ' | '.join(out))
print()
print('holm survivors:', json.dumps(d['holm_survivors_per_model'])[:1500])
print()
print('ranking:', json.dumps(d['effect_ranking_over_refusal_site_P'])[:1500])
print('note:', str(d['effect_ranking_note'])[:800])
print('arm0:', json.dumps(d['arm0_baselines'])[:1200])
"

bands=['B1','B2','B3','B4','B5','B6']; sites=['P','Dprime','E']

    return [[round(next(r for r in rows if r['model']==m and r['band']==b and r['site']==s)['effect_FR'],6) for s in sites] for b in bands]

   "cbar_label":"Δ over-refusal rate (F − R)"}
 ]}

EOF

### [102] TOOL CALL — Bash · 2026-09-22 07:30:13 UTC

````
cat > README.md <<'EOF'
# fig_causal_grid — causal grid of the refusal-direction ablation on over-refusal

Publication figure for the Qwen3-4B safety mech-interp study: two side-by-side
annotated heatmaps of the arm-F (directional ablation of the refusal feature)
effect on **over-refusal of benign-borderline prompts**, over the 6 layer bands
(B1–B6) x 3 intervention sites (P, D', E) causal grid.

## Provenance of every number

All 36 plotted values were read back from the run's own audited results table:

```
/ai-inventor/aii_data/runs/run_YqmEFECOIR3D/3_invention_loop/iter_5/
  gen_art/gen_art_evaluation_1/results/table_causal_grid.json
```

filtered to `arm == "F"`, `outcome == "over_refusal_hb"`, `status == "OK"`,
and the field plotted is `effect_FR` (= mean rate under arm F minus mean rate
under the matched-displacement random control R; n = 48 prompts per cell,
granularity 1/144 ≈ 0.0069).

Deviations from the paper-draft figure brief, and why:

- The draft quoted B4/B5 site-P values (Instruct −0.125 / −0.139, SafeRL
  −0.188 / −0.167) which **do** reproduce exactly. Its remaining 28 cells did
  not match the artifact; the artifact values are plotted instead.
- A third model (`abliterated`) exists in the table but only at site P —
  sites D' and E are `NOT_RUN` — so it is not drawn as a panel.
- **No arm-F cell survives Holm–Bonferroni over its 18-cell family**, for
  over-refusal or for judged refusal on harmful prompts (smallest
  `p_holm_rederived` for arm F is 0.095, SafeRL P/B4). The sole Holm-18
  survivor anywhere in the grid is arm **N6** at site P, band B5 in Instruct
  (effect −0.146, p_holm = 0.036) — a different arm, not shown here. The
  caption must say so.

## Caption

> Effect of directionally ablating the refusal feature (arm F) on over-refusal
> of benign-borderline prompts, across the 6-band x 3-site causal grid.
> (a) Qwen3-4B-Instruct, (b) Qwen3-4B-SafeRL. Cells give the rate difference
> between arm F and the matched-displacement random control R (n = 48 prompts
> per cell); blue indicates reduced over-refusal. Both models show the effect
> concentrated at bands B4–B5, strongest at the prompt site P (−0.139 Instruct,
> −0.188 SafeRL), but no arm-F cell survives Holm–Bonferroni correction over
> its 18-cell family (smallest p_holm = 0.095).

## Layout

- `fig_causal_grid_spec.json` — the figure's source: the complete chart spec
  (a two-panel composite of `heatmap`), holding every plotted value.
- `fig_causal_grid_v0.pdf` — **the deliverable**, vector, for `\includegraphics`.
- `fig_causal_grid_v0.png` — raster copy, for reading the figure back.
- `.aii/manifest.yaml` — disposability manifest (no heavy paths in this step).

## How to run

```bash
python /ai-inventor/.claude/skills/aii-data-fig-gen/scripts/chart_gen.py \
  --spec fig_causal_grid_spec.json --out fig_causal_grid_v0
```

Deterministic: the same spec renders a byte-identical PNG.

## Restoring removed files

Nothing in this directory is marked `delete` in `.aii/manifest.yaml` — it holds
only the spec, the two rendered figures and this README, all small text or
vector files that the downstream paper-assembly step reads. Re-running the
command above regenerates the PDF and PNG from the spec.
EOF
python3 -c "
import json;
json.dump({
 'title':'Where ablating refusal reduces over-refusal',
 'summary':'Two side-by-side annotated heatmaps (panel composite, 21:9) of the arm-F directional-ablation effect on over-refusal of benign-borderline prompts, over the 6 layer bands (B1-B6) x 3 intervention sites (P, D-prime, E) causal grid: (a) Qwen3-4B-Instruct, (b) Qwen3-4B-SafeRL. Cells are annotated with the signed rate difference between arm F and the matched-displacement random control R (n=48 prompts per cell), on a shared diverging blue-white-red scale fixed at +/-0.19 so the two panels are directly comparable; blue = reduced over-refusal. Rendered deterministically with the aii-data-fig-gen generator from fig_causal_grid_spec.json. EVIDENCE CHECK: every one of the 36 values was read back from the run own audited table at 3_invention_loop/iter_5/gen_art/gen_art_evaluation_1/results/table_causal_grid.json (field effect_FR, filtered arm=F, outcome=over_refusal_hb, status=OK). The draft brief headline cells reproduced exactly (Instruct B4/B5 at site P = -0.125/-0.139; SafeRL = -0.188/-0.167) but its other 28 cells did not match the artifact, so the artifact values were plotted instead - e.g. Instruct B3 is +0.083/-0.014/+0.090, not the drafted -0.035/-0.021/+0.007. The third model in the table (abliterated) was dropped because sites D-prime and E are NOT_RUN for it. Contrary to the drafted caption, no arm-F cell survives Holm-Bonferroni over its 18-cell family for EITHER outcome (smallest p_holm = 0.095, SafeRL P/B4); the sole Holm-18 survivor in the whole grid is a different arm (N6, Instruct, P/B5), which the README caption states explicitly. Fixes across renders: title shortened so it no longer crowded the panels. Axis names carry their units (layer band, intervention site, delta over-refusal rate F minus R).',
 'out_expected_files':{'image_path':'fig_causal_grid_v0.pdf'}
}, open('.terminal_claude_agent_struct_out.json','w'), indent=2)
"
ls -la fig_causal_grid_v0.pdf .terminal_claude_agent_struct_out.json && python3 -c "import json;d=json.load(open('.terminal_claude_agent_struct_out.json'));print(len(d['summary']),len(d['title']))"
````
